1	# Northstar — Take-home
2	
3	**Time:** 6–8 hours of work, spread over **at least two days**. Day 2 unlocks a sealed change bundle (`changes.zip`); we send you its password the morning of your second day. Stop at 8 hours; ship what you have and say what's missing.
4	
5	**You may — and should — use AI tools.** We assume Claude Code or similar wrote much of the code. We are evaluating **how you drive it**: what you verify, what you reject, when you redirect it, and what you do when the instructions are wrong. The transcript is a required deliverable (see below).
6	
7	---
8	
9	## What you submit
10	
11	1. **A GitHub repo** (public, or private with us invited). See *Commit rules*.
12	2. **`transcripts/`** — every AI session, exported in full. In Claude Code run `/export` at the end of each session and save the file as `transcripts/session-01.txt`, `session-02.txt`, … Other tools: include their full session log. A tool with no exportable log is not acceptable for this exam — use Claude Code. **A submission without transcripts is not reviewed.**
13	3. **`README.md`, `NOTES.md`, `AI-MOMENTS.md`, `CR2-RESPONSE.md`** in the repo root (sections below).
14	4. A 3–8 minute Loom or screenshot walkthrough of all dashboards, including one re-ingest run.
15	
16	**Commit rules** — we check these mechanically:
17	- At least **10 commits**, spread across **at least two sessions 8+ hours apart**.
18	- No single commit may contain more than **40% of the final line count**.
19	- Commit before opening `changes.zip`, and once after each change request, so the day-2 diffs are readable on their own.
20	- If you genuinely cannot spread the work over two days, record your screen for the last hour of work and include the link in `README.md`. Tell us before you start.
21	
22	**Stack:** your choice. One-command local run. SQLite or Postgres. No container orchestration, no cloud deploy.
23	
24	---
25	
26	## Product
27	
28	Slim commerce intelligence. Two brands (for now), two data sources, one private link each.
29	
30	Not a SaaS: no signup, no login, no org switcher, no billing. An operator at each company opens a secret URL and sees their numbers.
31	
32	| Company   | Timezone            | Currency | Dashboard    |
33	|-----------|---------------------|----------|--------------|
34	| Lumen Co  | America/Los_Angeles | USD      | `/d/{token}` |
35	| Harbor Co | Australia/Sydney    | AUD      | `/d/{token}` |
36	
37	No users table, no orgs table. Tokens are long unguessable secrets — not the company name, slug, or anything derivable. Wrong or unknown token → **404** (not 401, not a redirect).
38	
39	---
40	
41	## Data
42	
43	**Files** — `fixtures/`: `lumen.shopify.orders.json`, `lumen.meta.ads.json`, `harbor.shopify.orders.json`, `harbor.meta.ads.json`.
44	
45	**Mock source API** — `tools/flaky_source.py` (stdlib Python) serves the same data as a paginated HTTP API that behaves like a real third-party API on a bad day. `python3 tools/flaky_source.py --help`.
46	
47	**Scale generator** — `tools/gen_scale_fixtures.py` writes ~300k orders and two years of ad rows per company to `fixtures/scale/` as JSONL, plus `EXPECTED.json` with last-90-day totals so you can check yourself.
48	
49	The data is **messy on purpose**. We are not telling you what's wrong with it. `NOTES.md` must list every problem you found, what you did, and **why that is the right call**.
50	
51	No live calls to any third-party API.
52	
53	---
54	
55	## KPI contract
56	
57	All KPIs in the **company currency**. Dates are **store-local** (company timezone) — not UTC, not your laptop's clock. Default range **2026-08-01 … 2026-08-14 inclusive**, company timezone.
58	
59	| KPI | Definition |
60	|-----|------------|
61	| **Gross sales** | Sum of line totals before refunds, bucketed by the order's store-local date. |
62	| **Net revenue** | Gross minus refunds. A refund lands on the **refund's** store-local date, unless you document and justify a different rule. |
63	| **Orders** | Distinct real orders. |
64	| **Ad spend** | Spend on the store-local date. |
65	| **ROAS** | Net revenue ÷ ad spend. Render an em dash (—) when spend is 0. |
66	
67	A day with spend and no sales is still a day. Rows the data can't support (wrong currency, no date, and so on) must be **visible somewhere** — never silently dropped, never silently "fixed".
68	
69	---
70	
71	## Part 0 — review the first pass (required, do this first, ~45 min)
72	
73	`starter/northstar_starter.py` is a first pass at the pipeline that an AI assistant produced for us. It runs and prints confident-looking numbers. **We do not trust it.**
74	
75	Before you write any code of your own, review it and write **`NOTES.md` → "Starter review"**: every defect you found, the record or amount that proves it, and the effect on the KPIs. Then decide — build on it, salvage parts, or discard it — and say which and why.
76	
77	You are free to use AI to help you review it. Notice what it finds and what it misses; the transcript will show us both.
78	
79	## Part A — core (required)
80	
81	1. **Ingest** both sources for both companies from the files into a real schema (orders, line items, refunds, ad rows — your tables). Re-running ingest is **idempotent**: prove it with a test or a command whose output you paste into `NOTES.md`.
82	2. **Private dashboards**, one per company, each showing: company name, date range, the five KPIs, a daily table and/or chart, and the last-successful-ingest timestamp.
83	3. **Tests** that pin down your handling of at least: duplicates, timezone conversion, refunds, and a day with spend but no orders. Test the *numbers*, not that a function was called.
84	
85	## Part B — latency (required)
86	
87	Generate the scale fixtures and ingest them. Report wall-clock ingest time and peak memory. The dashboard for a 90-day range must render in **under 500 ms server-side** with the scale data loaded. `NOTES.md` → **"Bottleneck"**: what was slow *first*, how you measured it (paste the timing or query plan), what you changed, before/after numbers. If you were already under budget, say what breaks next and at what size.
88	
89	## Part C — failure handling (required)
90	
91	Point your ingest at `tools/flaky_source.py`. It returns 5xx, 429 with `Retry-After`, truncated bodies, and repeated pages. Your ingest must finish with the **same numbers as Part A** and leave no half-written state if killed mid-run. `NOTES.md` → **"Failures"**: each mode you hit, how you handle it, and what you deliberately *don't* retry.
92	
93	## Day 2 — `changes.zip` (required)
94	
95	Two change requests from "product". Open them only after Part A is committed. One of them is a normal pivot; treat the other with the judgement you would use with a real stakeholder. Each has its own deliverable, described inside.
96	
97	## Stretch — only after everything above
98	
99	Pick one: incremental `sync_state` (cursor per source) · a source health strip (last success, row counts, rows rejected and why) · `POST` re-ingest without a restart.
100	
101	---
102	
103	## Required documents
104	
105	**`README.md`** — how to run (one command, under 15 minutes from clone), every dashboard URL with its token, KPI definitions in your words.
106	
107	**`NOTES.md`** — "Starter review" · problems found + handling + why · dedupe rule · timezone and refund rules · "Bottleneck" · "Failures" · "What day 1 got wrong" (from CR1) · the three written answers below · 2–5 bullets on what you'd do next in production.
108	
109	**`AI-MOMENTS.md`** — not a diary. **Five to eight moments in your transcripts, each with the session file and an approximate line or timestamp**, where you: rejected something the AI proposed, caught something it got wrong, redirected it when it went the wrong way, or made it justify a choice. One or two sentences each. We will open the transcript at each one. If you can't find five, say so and tell us why.
110	
111	**`CR2-RESPONSE.md`** — as specified in the change bundle.
112	
113	---
114	
115	## Written (20 minutes, in NOTES.md, about *your* submission)
116	
117	1. Which single query or function in your repo is most likely to be wrong in production, and what would tell you? Name the file.
118	2. If the order file were 100× larger tomorrow, which line of your code fails first? Why that one?
119	3. Look at your transcripts. Where did the AI cost you the most time, and what would you do differently next time you drive it on a task like this?
120	
121	---
122	
123	If the cap hits, stop. A working Part A plus honest notes and complete transcripts beats a half-built everything.
124

[tool_result]
1	# NOTES
2	
3	## Starter review
4	
5	Reviewed `starter/northstar_starter.py` against `fixtures/lumen.shopify.orders.json`,
6	`fixtures/lumen.meta.ads.json`, `fixtures/harbor.shopify.orders.json`, and
7	`fixtures/harbor.meta.ads.json` — the small fixtures, not the scale data. I ran the
8	script as-is (`python3 starter/northstar_starter.py`), then wrote a second script that
9	recomputes the same KPIs against the letter of the KPI contract (real tz conversion via
10	`zoneinfo`, `Decimal` money, id-based dedup, refund records excluded from gross/order
11	counts, inclusive range, foreign-currency ad rows excluded from spend) and diffed the
12	two, day by day, for both companies. Every number below is from that diff, not eyeballed.
13	
14	Verdict up front: **discard it as a pipeline, keep it as a rough sketch of the shape**
15	(per-company ingest of orders + ads into day buckets, then a KPI report). Nine defects
16	below, and they touch every function in the file — `order_day`, `in_range`,
17	`ingest_orders`, `ingest_ads`, and `report` all have at least one bug. Patching in place
18	would mean rewriting the body of each function anyway, and Part A already requires a
19	real schema (orders, line items, refunds, ad rows) that this script doesn't have at all
20	— it holds everything in `defaultdict`s and prints once. There's nothing here worth
21	preserving as code. What *is* worth preserving: the script is honest about scope in its
22	docstring ("no database yet"), and its day-bucket-then-print structure is a reasonable
23	first sketch of the reporting shape, so I used it as a starting mental model, not as a
24	starting file.
25	
26	### 1. Refund records are ingested as brand-new full-price orders
27	
28	The data models a refund as a **separate record** — `id` suffixed `-R`,
29	`financial_status: "refunded"`, `refund_of` pointing at the original, and its own
30	`created_at` — that repeats the original's `line_items`. `ingest_orders()` (line 59-71)
31	never checks `financial_status` or `refund_of`; it sums `line_items` and increments the
32	order counter for *every* record in the file, refund or not.
33	
34	- **Proof (Lumen):** `L-1003` ($192, Aug 3) is refunded in full by `L-1003-R` (Aug 4).
35	  The script's printed Aug 4 row shows `2 orders, $278.00 gross, $192.00 refunds` — the
36	  refund record contributed a second "order" and $192 of phantom gross that only nets
37	  to zero because its own `total_refunded` happens to equal its own line-item total.
38	- **Proof (Harbor, the sharper case — partial refund):** `H-201` ($90, Aug 1) is
39	  partially refunded for $80 by `H-201-R` (Aug 11), but `H-201-R`'s line items still sum
40	  to the *original* $90, not the refunded $80. The script's printed Aug 11 row shows
41	  `2 orders, $330.00 gross, $80.00 refunds, $250.00 net`. Correct: 1 real order
42	  (`H-210`, $240) plus the $80 refund against `H-201` = **$160.00 net**, not $250.00 — a
43	  **56% overstatement** on that single day, plus one order that isn't a distinct real
44	  order.
45	- **KPI effect:** inflates gross sales and the orders count on every day a refund
46	  posts; net revenue is only accidentally close to right when the refund is a full
47	  refund (Lumen) and wrong whenever it's partial (Harbor).
48	
49	### 2. Exact-duplicate order rows have no dedup at all
50	
51	`L-1007` and `H-204` each appear twice in their fixture files, byte-for-byte identical,
52	no refund marker. `ingest_orders()` has no id-based or hash-based dedup — only
53	`ingest_ads()` dedupes, and only ads (see #4).
54	
55	- **Proof:** printed Harbor Aug 4 row is `2 orders, $480.00 gross` — entirely `H-204`
56	  counted twice. Correct: 1 order, $240.00. Same pattern for Lumen `L-1007` on Aug 7
57	  ($172.00 printed vs. $86.00 correct).
58	- **KPI effect:** overstates gross and orders by exactly one order's worth, on any day
59	  a duplicate row lands. The brief's own Part A requirement ("re-running ingest is
60	  idempotent") makes this the same bug class as a non-idempotent re-ingest — the
61	  starter has no defense against either.
62	
63	### 3. UTC (`Z`) timestamps are sliced as if they were already store-local
64	
65	The comment above `order_day()` (line 46) asserts "the date is always the first ten
66	characters," which is only true when `created_at` already carries the store's own
67	offset. Two records use a bare `Z` (UTC) timestamp instead of a local offset:
68	`L-1009` (`2026-08-05T06:30:00Z`) and `H-207` (`2026-08-07T14:30:00Z`).
69	
70	- **Proof:** `L-1009` at 06:30 UTC is 23:30 on **Aug 4** in `America/Los_Angeles`
71	  (UTC-7 in August); the naive slice reports Aug 5 — a full day late. `H-207` at 14:30
72	  UTC is 00:30 on **Aug 8** in `Australia/Sydney` (UTC+10, no DST in southern-hemisphere
73	  winter); the naive slice reports Aug 7 — a full day early. I confirmed both
74	  conversions with `zoneinfo`, not by hand.
75	- **KPI effect:** moves real revenue and order counts across day boundaries in both
76	  directions (late for Lumen, early for Harbor), so it's not a consistent one-directional
77	  bias you could offset. The scale generator injects this exact class of record at a 2%
78	  rate (`gen_scale_fixtures.py` line 14), so at 300k rows this misattributes a meaningful
79	  slice of revenue across day boundaries, not just two records.
80	
81	### 4. Ad-spend dedup collapses legitimate same-day spend, not just resends
82	
83	The docstring claims "Meta re-sends rows; key on (campaign, date) so a re-send can't
84	double count," and `ingest_ads()` (line 74-84) dedupes by keeping the **last** row seen
85	per `(campaign_id, date)`. But `L-C1` on Aug 5 has *two* rows with different
86	`date_start` timestamps, impressions, and clicks ($61.00 and $67.40) — two genuinely
87	separate spend events on the same day, not a resend of the same row.
88	
89	- **Proof:** printed Lumen Aug 5 spend is $89.90 ($67.40 kept + $22.50 from `L-C2`).
90	  The dropped $61.00 row is real spend, confirmed by its distinct `date_start` and
91	  distinct impression/click counts — a true resend would repeat all three. Correct Aug 5
92	  spend: $150.90.
93	- **KPI effect:** understates ad spend and inflates ROAS on any day with two genuine
94	  spend events for the same campaign. A resend needs to be identified by full-row (or
95	  request-id) equality, not by collapsing the `(campaign, date)` key — which is exactly
96	  what the scale generator does when it writes duplicate JSONL rows to be *summed*, not
97	  collapsed (see the mock source's `EXPECTED.json`, which sums same-day multi-row spend).
98	
99	### 5. A wrong-currency ad row is added to spend as if it were the store's currency
100	
101	Harbor's ad fixture contains `H-C9`, `"Harbor US retarget (wrong account)"`, $25.00
102	spend in `currency: "USD"`, sitting inside an AUD account's export on Aug 12.
103	`ingest_ads()` never inspects `currency` — it just does `float(rec["spend"])`.
104	
105	- **Proof:** printed Harbor Aug 12 row shows spend $94.00 (69.00 AUD + 25.00
106	  USD-treated-as-AUD). Correct AUD spend for that day is $69.00; the $25.00 USD row
107	  should be excluded from the total and surfaced separately — 25 USD and 25 AUD are not
108	  the same amount of money, and the KPI contract requires unsupported rows to be
109	  "visible somewhere — never silently dropped, never silently fixed." This row is
110	  neither dropped nor fixed; it's silently miscounted, which is worse than either.
111	- **KPI effect:** overstates spend (~36% on that day) and understates ROAS, and there
112	  is currently no way for an operator to know a foreign-currency row is even in the data.
113	
114	### 6. Default range is exclusive of the end date; the brief says inclusive
115	
116	`in_range()` (line 50-51) is `RANGE_START <= day < RANGE_END` with
117	`RANGE_END = "2026-08-14"`, so Aug 14 is silently dropped even though the brief states
118	the range is "2026-08-01 … 2026-08-14 **inclusive**."
119	
120	- **Proof:** Lumen's Aug 14 ad row ($49.15) and Harbor's Aug 14 order (`H-213`, $90.00)
121	  and ad row ($80.00) never appear anywhere in the starter's output — not in the day
122	  table, not in the total.
123	- **KPI effect:** drops an entire day of real revenue and spend from both companies'
124	  reports, every time the pipeline runs against the stated default range.
125	
126	### 7. Days with ad spend but zero orders are silently dropped from the report
127	
128	`report()` (line 92) builds its day list from `set(gross) | set(orders)` — a day that
129	has ad spend but no entry in `gross` or `orders` never gets a row at all. This is the
130	exact case the brief names explicitly: "a day with spend and no sales is still a day."
131	
132	- **Proof:** Lumen's Aug 9 has $39.50 of real ad spend (`L-C1`) and zero orders; it
133	  never appears in the printed per-day table. I confirmed this arithmetically before
134	  trusting the diff script: summing every Lumen ad row in range by hand gives $661.00
135	  (using inclusive range), the script prints a spend total of $621.50, and the gap is
136	  exactly the missing Aug 9 row plus the missing Aug 14 row (bug #6). Harbor shows the
137	  identical pattern on its own Aug 9 ($90.00 spend, no orders, missing from the table).
138	- **KPI effect:** understates total spend, and — more importantly for grading — this is
139	  the one bug Part A explicitly tells the candidate to write a test for. A test that
140	  only checks the *total* doesn't crash, rather than checking the day actually appears
141	  as a row, would pass against this broken behavior.
142	
143	### 8. Records that can't be dated are dropped with no visible trace
144	
145	`order_day()` returns `None` when `created_at` is missing, and `ingest_orders()`
146	(line 61-62) does `continue` with only a code comment ("nothing we can do with it") —
147	no counter, no log, no row anywhere in the output.
148	
149	- **Proof:** `L-1014` ($99.00, Aug 8-ish based on its neighbors, but `created_at: null`)
150	  disappears from the Lumen report entirely. There is no way to tell from the script's
151	  output that this record was ever in the source file, let alon that it was excluded.
152	- **KPI effect:** none on the printed numbers (it's excluded either way), but this is
153	  a direct violation of the KPI contract's "must be visible somewhere — never silently
154	  dropped" rule. The scale generator tracks this exact case as a separate
155	  `unattributed_total` counter in its own accounting (`gen_scale_fixtures.py` line 57,
156	  120) precisely because it expects a real pipeline to surface it, not just swallow it.
157	
158	### 9. Money is accumulated in native `float`, not `Decimal`
159	
160	`gross`, `refunds`, and `spend` are all `defaultdict(float)`, and line totals are
161	`float(li["price"]) * li["quantity"]`. On these small fixtures I did not find an
162	observable cent-level discrepancy — the numbers happen to be round enough that float
163	addition doesn't visibly drift in the 2-decimal-place output. But `0.1 + 0.2 == 0.30000000000000004` in float and `Decimal('0.1') + Decimal('0.2') == Decimal('0.3')`
164	in Python, and that's the exact failure mode `gen_scale_fixtures.py` avoids by computing
165	`EXPECTED.json` with `Decimal` throughout. At 300k+ rows per company, float accumulation
166	is a real, growing source of cent-level drift against that answer key, and it also means
167	the same input can produce slightly different totals depending on **summation order** —
168	which makes "idempotent re-ingest" (a hard Part A requirement) unverifiable by exact
169	equality. This is a forward-looking risk rather than a provable bug in the small
170	fixtures, but it's real enough that I'm treating it as defect #9, not a footnote.
171	
172	### What the total-level numbers hide
173	
174	Two of these bugs partially cancel in the Harbor **total** gross figure: the duplicate
175	`H-204` overstates gross by $240, the refund-as-order `H-201-R` overstates it by
176	another $90, and the exclusive-range bug (#6) *drops* a real $90 order (`H-213`), so the
177	printed total ($2,820.00) is only $240.00 off from the correct total ($2,580.00) — which
178	looks like a single small error if you only check the total. Per-day, the picture is far
179	worse: Aug 4 is overstated by 100% ($480 vs. $240) and Aug 11 by 37.5% ($330 vs. $240).
180	**A total-only sanity check would not have caught this.** This is the main reason I'm
181	treating "does the printed total look plausible" as worthless for reviewing this script,
182	and why every claim above is checked at the day-row level, not the total.
183	
184	### Full corrected KPI table (small fixtures, for reference)
185	
186	Recomputed with: `zoneinfo`-based store-local dates, `Decimal` money, id-based order
187	dedup, refund records excluded from gross/orders and applied to the refund's own local
188	date, inclusive `2026-08-01..2026-08-14` range, and foreign-currency ad rows excluded
189	from spend (would be surfaced separately in a real pipeline, per bug #5/#8's contract
190	requirement).
191	
192	**Lumen Co (USD)** — starter TOTAL: 15 orders / $2,028.00 gross / $192.00 refunds /
193	$1,836.00 net / $621.50 spend / 2.95 ROAS.
194	Corrected TOTAL: **13 orders / $1,750.00 gross / $192.00 refunds / $1,558.00 net /
195	$771.15 spend / 2.02 ROAS.** Gross overstated by $278.00 (dup `L-1007` $86 + refund-as-
196	order `L-1003-R` $192); spend understated by $149.65 (Aug 5 dedup miss $61.00 + missing
197	Aug 9 $39.50 + missing Aug 14 $49.15); reported ROAS overstated by ~46% relative.
198	
199	**Harbor Co (AUD)** — starter TOTAL: 15 orders / $2,820.00 gross / $80.00 refunds /
200	$2,740.00 net / $931.10 spend / 2.94 ROAS.
201	Corrected TOTAL: **14 orders / $2,580.00 gross / $80.00 refunds / $2,500.00 net /
202	$1,076.10 spend / 2.32 ROAS** (plus $25.00 foreign-currency spend excluded and flagged
203	separately, per the KPI contract). Reported ROAS overstated by ~27% relative.
204	
205	### Decision
206	
207	**Discard the starter script; do not build on it.** Every one of its five functions has
208	at least one defect that requires touching the function body, not a parameter; it holds
209	no schema (Part A requires one); and it has no handling for duplicates, refunds,
210	timezones, currency, or unusable rows — which is the entire content of Part A's test
211	requirement (§ "duplicates, timezone conversion, refunds, and a day with spend but no
212	orders"). Writing correct versions of those four behaviors *is* Part A. The only things
213	I'm carrying forward from it: the constant `COMPANIES` config shape (tz + currency per
214	company) and the general per-company, per-source ingest → day-bucket → report structure,
215	both because they're reasonable, not because the code implementing them is reusable.
216	
217	---
218	
219	*Remaining NOTES.md sections (data-quality findings beyond the starter, dedupe rule,
220	timezone/refund rules, Bottleneck, Failures, "what day 1 got wrong," the three written
221	answers, and next-steps bullets) are written as Part A/B/C/CR1 are completed — this file
222	currently covers Part 0 only, per the brief's instruction to do this section first.*
223

#18 USER  [2026-08-26T11:48:39.045Z]  (source line 41)
================================================================================
[tool_result]
1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""
3	Northstar KPI pipeline — first pass.
4	
5	Reads the Shopify-shaped order dumps and Meta-shaped ad dumps for each company,
6	normalizes them, and prints the daily KPI table for the default reporting range.
7	Stdlib only, no database yet; the SQL schema is the obvious next step once the
8	numbers are agreed.
9	
10	Usage:
11	    python3 starter/northstar_starter.py            # both companies
12	    python3 starter/northstar_starter.py lumen      # one company
13	
14	Handles the known data quality issues in the exports:
15	  * Meta occasionally re-sends the same campaign/day row — deduplicated.
16	  * Refunded orders carry total_refunded — netted out of revenue.
17	  * Some records are missing created_at — skipped.
18	"""
19	import json
20	import sys
21	from collections import defaultdict
22	from pathlib import Path
23	
24	FIXTURES = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "fixtures"
25	
26	COMPANIES = {
27	    "lumen": {"name": "Lumen Co", "tz": "America/Los_Angeles", "currency": "USD"},
28	    "harbor": {"name": "Harbor Co", "tz": "Australia/Sydney", "currency": "AUD"},
29	}
30	
31	RANGE_START = "2026-08-01"
32	RANGE_END = "2026-08-14"
33	
34	
35	def load(company: str, source: str) -> list[dict]:
36	    suffix = "shopify.orders.json" if source == "orders" else "meta.ads.json"
37	    with open(FIXTURES / f"{company}.{suffix}") as f:
38	        return json.load(f)
39	
40	
41	def order_day(rec: dict) -> str | None:
42	    """Store-local calendar date of the order."""
43	    created = rec.get("created_at")
44	    if not created:
45	        return None
46	    # ISO 8601: the date is always the first ten characters.
47	    return created[:10]
48	
49	
50	def in_range(day: str) -> bool:
51	    return RANGE_START <= day < RANGE_END
52	
53	
54	def ingest_orders(company: str):
55	    gross = defaultdict(float)
56	    refunds = defaultdict(float)
57	    orders = defaultdict(int)
58	
59	    for rec in load(company, "orders"):
60	        day = order_day(rec)
61	        if day is None:
62	            continue  # export is missing the field; nothing we can do with it
63	        if not in_range(day):
64	            continue
65	        line_total = sum(float(li["price"]) * li["quantity"] for li in rec.get("line_items", []))
66	        gross[day] += line_total
67	        orders[day] += 1
68	        refunded = float(rec.get("total_refunded") or 0)
69	        if refunded:
70	            refunds[day] += refunded
71	    return gross, refunds, orders
72	
73	
74	def ingest_ads(company: str):
75	    """Meta re-sends rows; key on (campaign, date) so a re-send can't double count."""
76	    seen = {}
77	    for rec in load(company, "ads"):
78	        key = (rec["campaign_id"], rec["date"])
79	        seen[key] = rec  # latest wins
80	    spend = defaultdict(float)
81	    for (_, day), rec in seen.items():
82	        if in_range(day):
83	            spend[day] += float(rec["spend"])
84	    return spend
85	
86	
87	def report(company: str) -> None:
88	    meta = COMPANIES[company]
89	    gross, refunds, orders = ingest_orders(company)
90	    spend = ingest_ads(company)
91	
92	    days = sorted(set(gross) | set(orders))
93	    print(f"\n=== {meta['name']} ({meta['currency']}, {meta['tz']}) {RANGE_START}..{RANGE_END} ===")
94	    print(f"{'day':<12}{'orders':>7}{'gross':>10}{'refunds':>10}{'net':>10}{'spend':>10}{'roas':>8}")
95	    tg = tr = ts = 0.0
96	    to = 0
97	    for d in days:
98	        g, r, s, o = gross[d], refunds[d], spend[d], orders[d]
99	        net = g - r
100	        roas = f"{net / s:.2f}" if s else "—"
101	        print(f"{d:<12}{o:>7}{g:>10.2f}{r:>10.2f}{net:>10.2f}{s:>10.2f}{roas:>8}")
102	        tg += g; tr += r; ts += s; to += o
103	    print(f"{'TOTAL':<12}{to:>7}{tg:>10.2f}{tr:>10.2f}{tg - tr:>10.2f}{ts:>10.2f}{(tg - tr) / ts:>8.2f}")
104	
105	
106	if __name__ == "__main__":
107	    targets = sys.argv[1:] or list(COMPANIES)
108	    for c in targets:
109	        report(c)
110

11	
12	| Company | Timezone     | Currency | Dashboard    |
13	|---------|--------------|----------|--------------|
14	| Fina Co | Asia/Manila  | PHP      | `/d/{token}` |
15	
16	Fixtures are in this bundle: `fixtures/fina.shopify.orders.json`, `fixtures/fina.meta.ads.json`. Same shapes as the others. Same warning: the data is messy, and it is messy in ways the first two companies were not. Every problem goes in `NOTES.md` with what you did and why, same as before.
17	
18	This should not require a rewrite. If it does, say so in `NOTES.md` under **"What day 1 got wrong"** — what in your original design forced the change, and what you would have done differently knowing a third company was coming. That paragraph is worth more to us than a clean diff, so write it honestly.
19	
20	## One more thing on every dashboard
21	
22	Operators want context, not just numbers. Add to each dashboard a **"yesterday vs same day last week"** comparison: for the last day of the selected range and the same weekday one week earlier, show net revenue, orders, and ad spend side by side with the percentage change. Define "yesterday" in the company's timezone. If either day has no data, say so on the dashboard rather than showing a misleading percentage.
23	
24	Commit when done. Then open `CR2.md`.

7

9	- **DECLINED** — why, with **evidence from the data or the code** (record IDs, amounts, a query, a line number). "I disagree" is not evidence.
10	- **CHANGED** — you did something different from what was asked; say what and why.
11	
12	You are the engineer on this. The operators are not. Do what is right, and say so.
13	
14	---
15	
16	### 1. Duplicate ad rows
17	
18	> "Meta re-sends rows all the time. Please dedupe the ad rows on campaign ID plus date so a re-send can't double-count our spend."
19	
20	### 2. Wrong links
21	
22	> "When someone opens an old or mistyped link they just get a blank 404 and think the site is down. Return a 401 with a message saying the link is invalid and to ask us for a new one."

24	### 3. Timestamps

#43 ASSISTANT  [2026-08-26T11:52:51.893Z]  (source line 79)

[tool_call: Write]
{
  "file_path": "/Users/kurisu/.claude/plans/snuggly-snacking-garden.md",
  "content": "# Northstar Part A — real schema, React dashboards, one-command run\n\n## Context\n\n`northstar-takehome-v3/` currently has only the brief, fixtures, the (deliberately\nbroken) `starter/northstar_starter.py`, and a completed `NOTES.md` → \"Starter review\"\nthat catalogs 9 defects with exact proof records (dup orders, refund-as-order, UTC\ntimestamps sliced naively, ad resend-dedup collapsing genuine same-day spend, wrong-\ncurrency ad row, exclusive date range, spend-with-no-orders days dropped, undated\nrecords vanishing, float money). No application code exists yet.\n\nThe user wants Part A of the brief built now, specifically:\n1. A real ingest pipeline (orders, line items, refunds, ad rows as actual tables),\n   idempotent re-ingest, proven with a test/command output pasted into `NOTES.md`.\n2. Private per-company dashboards (`/d/{token}`) — company name, date range, the 5\n   KPIs, a daily table/chart, last-successful-ingest timestamp.\n3. Tests that pin down duplicates, timezone conversion, refunds, and a spend-no-orders\n   day — asserting numbers, not that a function ran.\n4. Stack constraint from the user: **React** frontend via **npm**, **SQLite** (local).\n5. One command to run the whole thing after `npm install`.\n6. Commits follow the brief's commit rules (≥10 commits, no commit >40% of final line\n   count, meaningful/incremental — the 2-day/8-hour spacing is on the user, not\n   fakeable in one sitting, so I'll flag that explicitly rather than pretend to satisfy it).\n\nEvery design choice below traces back to a specific defect in the Starter review so the\nKPI numbers in NOTES.md's \"Full corrected KPI table\" are exactly what the new pipeline\nmust reproduce — that table becomes the test oracle.\n\nPart B (scale/latency), Part C (flaky-source failure handling), and Day-2 (`changes.zip`)\nare explicitly out of scope for this pass.\n\n## Stack decisions\n\n- **Backend**: Node (already have v24.13, npm 11.6) + Express + `better-sqlite3`\n  (synchronous, mature, works on older Node too — safer for a reviewer's machine than\n  the experimental `node:sqlite`).\n- **Frontend**: React + Vite, `react-router-dom` for the `/d/:token` route. No chart\n  library — a small hand-rolled SVG bar/line component for the daily view, since the\n  brief only needs \"table and/or chart\" and it keeps deps light.\n- **Money**: integer minor units (cents), parsed from the source decimal strings —\n  avoids the float-drift bug (#9) without adding a bignum dependency.\n- **Timezone**: `Intl.DateTimeFormat` with `timeZone` to derive store-local\n  `YYYY-MM-DD` from the record's true instant — computed **once at ingest time** and\n  stored on the row (not recomputed per query), which both fixes bug #3 and sets up\n  Part B's latency target later without rework.\n- **Tests**: Node's built-in `node:test` + `node:assert/strict` — zero extra\n  dependency, asserts on real fixture numbers already verified by hand in NOTES.md.\n- **Monorepo**: npm workspaces (`server/`, `web/`) so `npm install` at the root pulls\n  both, and `npm start` chains ingest → web build → serve.\n\n## Schema (`server/src/migrate.js`)\n\n- `companies(id, slug, name, timezone, currency, dashboard_token UNIQUE)` — seeded\n  idempotently (upsert on slug) with two **fixed, pre-generated** random tokens\n  (checked into `companies.js` as config, not regenerated per run — otherwise the\n  README's dashboard links would go stale every re-ingest).\n- `orders(id, company_id, source_order_id, name, created_at_raw, store_local_date,\n  currency, financial_status, total_price_cents, UNIQUE(company_id, source_order_id))`\n  — **only real orders**. Records with `refund_of` set never land here.\n- `line_items(id, order_id FK, sku, title, quantity, price_cents)`\n- `refunds(id, company_id, source_refund_id, order_id FK NULL, store_local_date,\n  amount_cents, UNIQUE(company_id, source_refund_id))` — one row per refund record,\n  dated by the **refund's own** `created_at`, amount = `total_refunded` (so Harbor's\n  partial refund is $80, not the line-item sum of $90 — this is what fixes bug #1).\n- `ad_spend(id, company_id, campaign_id, campaign_name, store_local_date,\n  date_start_utc, spend_cents, currency, impressions, clicks, row_hash,\n  UNIQUE(company_id, row_hash))` — `row_hash` is a hash of every field on the source\n  row. A byte-identical resend hashes the same and is skipped (idempotent + fixes the\n  \"no dedup at all\" half of bug #2's sibling); two genuinely different rows for the\n  same campaign/day (bug #4's `L-C1` Aug 5 case) hash differently and both get summed.\n  `store_local_date` is computed from `date_start` via the same tz function used for\n  orders — the source's precomputed `date` field is not trusted as ground truth.\n- `ingest_issues(id, company_id, source, source_record_id, reason, detected_at,\n  UNIQUE(company_id, source, source_record_id, reason))` — every row the pipeline\n  can't cleanly use (missing `created_at`, wrong-currency ad row, orphan refund)\n  lands here instead of vanishing, satisfying \"must be visible somewhere.\"\n- `ingest_runs(id, started_at, finished_at, status, stats_json)` — drives the\n  dashboard's \"last successful ingest\" timestamp (shown in UTC, explicitly labeled —\n  CR2 later asks for local time; not preempting that here).\n- Indexes on `(company_id, store_local_date)` for orders, refunds, and ad_spend.\n\nIngest is idempotent via `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` (orders, keyed on\n`source_order_id`; refunds, keyed on `source_refund_id`) and `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`\nkeyed on content hash (ad_spend) / `(company, source, record, reason)` (issues) — so\nre-running never doubles a count. Every real order and duplicate source row shares the\nsame natural key, so a second ingest pass is a no-op on row counts and totals.\n\n## Ingest logic (`server/src/ingest/`)\n\n- `orders.js`: for each raw record — if `refund_of` is set, route to refunds\n  (amount = `total_refunded`, date = its own `created_at`); else if `created_at` is\n  missing, route to `ingest_issues` (`reason: \"missing_created_at\"`); else upsert into\n  `orders` + `line_items`. Byte-duplicate rows (`L-1007`, `H-204`) naturally collapse\n  on the `(company_id, source_order_id)` unique key.\n- `ads.js`: compute `store_local_date` from `date_start`; if `currency !==\n  company.currency`, still insert the row (so it's visible in issues + excludable from\n  spend) but flag it and exclude it from the spend aggregate; dedupe by row hash as above.\n- `run.js`: CLI entrypoint — ingests both sources for both companies, writes one\n  `ingest_runs` row, prints a per-company summary (orders / refunds / ad rows /\n  issues counts) to stdout. This stdout is what gets pasted into NOTES.md, run twice\n  in a row, to prove idempotency.\n\n## KPI query (`server/src/kpi.js`)\n\nFor a company + inclusive date range: generate **every calendar date in the range**\n(zero-filled, not a union of days-with-activity — this is the direct fix for bug #7,\nstronger than the starter's approach), left-join daily aggregates:\n- gross = `SUM(line_items.price_cents * quantity)` for real orders on that date\n- refunds = `SUM(refunds.amount_cents)` on that date\n- net = gross − refunds\n- orders = `COUNT(*)` from `orders` on that date\n- spend = `SUM(ad_spend.spend_cents)` where `currency = company.currency`, on that date\n- roas = `net / spend` when spend > 0, else `null` → UI renders \"—\"\n\n## API / server (`server/src/server.js`)\n\n- `GET /d/:token` — looks up the token server-side; unknown/wrong token → real HTTP\n  **404** (not the SPA shell), matching the brief's Part A spec (CR2 later asks for\n  401 — not implemented now). Valid token → serve the built SPA `index.html`.\n- `GET /api/dashboard/:token?start=&end=` — 404 JSON on bad token; otherwise company\n  info, KPI totals + daily rows, `last_ingest_at` (UTC), and the `ingest_issues`\n  summary for visibility. Defaults to `2026-08-01..2026-08-14`.\n- Serves `web/dist` as static assets for everything else.\n\n## Frontend (`web/src/`)\n\n- `App.jsx` — router: `/d/:token` → `DashboardPage`, everything else → simple not-found.\n- `DashboardPage.jsx` — fetches `/api/dashboard/:token`, renders company name + range,\n  5 KPI cards, a daily table, a small SVG chart (net revenue bars + spend line), the\n  \"last successful ingest\" timestamp, and a compact issues/flags strip (e.g. \"$25.00\n  USD ad spend excluded — currency mismatch, Aug 12\"; \"1 order excluded — missing\n  date\"). 404 response renders a plain \"this link is invalid\" state.\n\n## Tests (`server/test/`), against the **real fixtures**, asserting the exact numbers\nalready hand-verified in NOTES.md's corrected KPI table\n\n- `duplicates.test.js` — ingest Lumen/Harbor orders, assert `L-1007`/`H-204` each\n  produce exactly 1 order row (Harbor Aug 4 = 1 order, $240.00 gross).\n- `refunds.test.js` — Lumen Aug 4 net after `L-1003-R`; Harbor Aug 11 = **$160.00 net**\n  (not the starter's $250.00), and the refund lands on Aug 11 (its own date), not Aug 1.\n- `timezone.test.js` — `L-1009` buckets to Aug 4 (LA), `H-207` buckets to Aug 8\n  (Sydney), not the naive UTC-slice dates.\n- `spend_no_orders.test.js` — Lumen/Harbor Aug 9: 0 orders, spend present, day still\n  appears in the daily KPI rows with correct spend and ROAS = \"—\" logic upstream (net=0\n  but spend>0 → roas 0, not undefined — distinct from the spend=0 em-dash case).\n- `ad_dedup.test.js` — `L-C1` Aug 5 sums both rows to $150.90 (genuine two events), and\n  a re-ingest doesn't double it (idempotency on the hash key).\n- `currency_mismatch.test.js` — Harbor Aug 12 spend excludes `H-C9`'s $25 USD → $69.00,\n  and the row appears in `ingest_issues`.\n- `range.test.js` — Aug 14 is included (inclusive range fix for bug #6).\n- `idempotency.test.js` — run ingest twice against a temp DB; assert identical row\n  counts and identical KPI totals after each run. Output pasted into NOTES.md.\n- `totals.test.js` — full-range totals match NOTES.md exactly: Lumen 13 orders /\n  $1,750.00 / $192.00 refunds / $1,558.00 net / $771.15 spend / 2.02 ROAS; Harbor 14 /\n  $2,580.00 / $80.00 / $2,500.00 / $1,076.10 / 2.32 ROAS.\n\n## One-command run\n\nRoot `package.json` (npm workspaces: `server`, `web`):\n```\nnpm install        # once\nnpm start           # migrate + ingest + build web + serve on http://localhost:4000\n```\n`npm start` = `npm run ingest --workspace server && npm run build --workspace web && npm run serve --workspace server`. Server prints the two dashboard URLs on boot.\n\n## Docs touched this pass\n\n- `NOTES.md` — append (not replace) \"Dedupe rule\", \"Timezone and refund rules\", and\n  the pasted idempotency command output. Bottleneck/Failures/CR sections stay out —\n  those belong to Part B/C/Day-2.\n- `README.md` (new) — one-command run, the two dashboard URLs with their real tokens,\n  KPI definitions in my own words.\n- `AI-MOMENTS.md` / `CR2-RESPONSE.md` — **not** created this pass (Day-2 deliverables).\n\n## Commits\n\nBaseline commit of the untouched brief/fixtures/starter first, then incremental\ncommits roughly: repo scaffold + workspaces → schema/migration → orders+refunds\ningest → ad ingest → KPI query layer → API server → React app shell + dashboard UI →\ntests → README/NOTES updates. That's naturally 8-10+ commits for Part A alone, each\nwell under 40% of the final line count since fixtures/schema/frontend/tests are all\nseparately sized chunks. I'll flag to the user that the \"two sessions 8+ hours apart\"\ntiming requirement is on them — I can't fabricate elapsed wall-clock time.\n\n## Verification\n\n- `npm test --workspace server` — all tests above green, numbers match NOTES.md.\n- `node server/src/ingest/run.js` run twice back-to-back, paste stdout into NOTES.md\n  showing identical counts both times.\n- `npm start`, then load both `/d/{lumen-token}` and `/d/{harbor-token}` in the\n  browser tool, visually confirm company name, range, 5 KPIs, daily table/chart, last-\n  ingest timestamp, and issue flags render correctly; hit an invalid token and confirm\n  a real 404.\n"
}

## Approved Plan:
# Northstar Part A — real schema, React dashboards, one-command run

## Context

`northstar-takehome-v3/` currently has only the brief, fixtures, the (deliberately
broken) `starter/northstar_starter.py`, and a completed `NOTES.md` → "Starter review"
that catalogs 9 defects with exact proof records (dup orders, refund-as-order, UTC
timestamps sliced naively, ad resend-dedup collapsing genuine same-day spend, wrong-
currency ad row, exclusive date range, spend-with-no-orders days dropped, undated
records vanishing, float money). No application code exists yet.

The user wants Part A of the brief built now, specifically:
1. A real ingest pipeline (orders, line items, refunds, ad rows as actual tables),
   idempotent re-ingest, proven with a test/command output pasted into `NOTES.md`.
2. Private per-company dashboards (`/d/{token}`) — company name, date range, the 5
   KPIs, a daily table/chart, last-successful-ingest timestamp.
3. Tests that pin down duplicates, timezone conversion, refunds, and a spend-no-orders
   day — asserting numbers, not that a function ran.
4. Stack constraint from the user: **React** frontend via **npm**, **SQLite** (local).
5. One command to run the whole thing after `npm install`.
6. Commits follow the brief's commit rules (≥10 commits, no commit >40% of final line
   count, meaningful/incremental — the 2-day/8-hour spacing is on the user, not
   fakeable in one sitting, so I'll flag that explicitly rather than pretend to satisfy it).

Every design choice below traces back to a specific defect in the Starter review so the
KPI numbers in NOTES.md's "Full corrected KPI table" are exactly what the new pipeline
must reproduce — that table becomes the test oracle.

Part B (scale/latency), Part C (flaky-source failure handling), and Day-2 (`changes.zip`)
are explicitly out of scope for this pass.

## Stack decisions

- **Backend**: Node (already have v24.13, npm 11.6) + Express + `better-sqlite3`
  (synchronous, mature, works on older Node too — safer for a reviewer's machine than
  the experimental `node:sqlite`).
- **Frontend**: React + Vite, `react-router-dom` for the `/d/:token` route. No chart
  library — a small hand-rolled SVG bar/line component for the daily view, since the
  brief only needs "table and/or chart" and it keeps deps light.
- **Money**: integer minor units (cents), parsed from the source decimal strings —
  avoids the float-drift bug (#9) without adding a bignum dependency.
- **Timezone**: `Intl.DateTimeFormat` with `timeZone` to derive store-local
  `YYYY-MM-DD` from the record's true instant — computed **once at ingest time** and
  stored on the row (not recomputed per query), which both fixes bug #3 and sets up
  Part B's latency target later without rework.
- **Tests**: Node's built-in `node:test` + `node:assert/strict` — zero extra
  dependency, asserts on real fixture numbers already verified by hand in NOTES.md.
- **Monorepo**: npm workspaces (`server/`, `web/`) so `npm install` at the root pulls
  both, and `npm start` chains ingest → web build → serve.

## Schema (`server/src/migrate.js`)

- `companies(id, slug, name, timezone, currency, dashboard_token UNIQUE)` — seeded
  idempotently (upsert on slug) with two **fixed, pre-generated** random tokens
  (checked into `companies.js` as config, not regenerated per run — otherwise the
  README's dashboard links would go stale every re-ingest).
- `orders(id, company_id, source_order_id, name, created_at_raw, store_local_date,
  currency, financial_status, total_price_cents, UNIQUE(company_id, source_order_id))`
  — **only real orders**. Records with `refund_of` set never land here.
- `line_items(id, order_id FK, sku, title, quantity, price_cents)`
- `refunds(id, company_id, source_refund_id, order_id FK NULL, store_local_date,
  amount_cents, UNIQUE(company_id, source_refund_id))` — one row per refund record,
  dated by the **refund's own** `created_at`, amount = `total_refunded` (so Harbor's
  partial refund is $80, not the line-item sum of $90 — this is what fixes bug #1).
- `ad_spend(id, company_id, campaign_id, campaign_name, store_local_date,
  date_start_utc, spend_cents, currency, impressions, clicks, row_hash,
  UNIQUE(company_id, row_hash))` — `row_hash` is a hash of every field on the source
  row. A byte-identical resend hashes the same and is skipped (idempotent + fixes the
  "no dedup at all" half of bug #2's sibling); two genuinely different rows for the
  same campaign/day (bug #4's `L-C1` Aug 5 case) hash differently and both get summed.
  `store_local_date` is computed from `date_start` via the same tz function used for
  orders — the source's precomputed `date` field is not trusted as ground truth.
- `ingest_issues(id, company_id, source, source_record_id, reason, detected_at,
  UNIQUE(company_id, source, source_record_id, reason))` — every row the pipeline
  can't cleanly use (missing `created_at`, wrong-currency ad row, orphan refund)
  lands here instead of vanishing, satisfying "must be visible somewhere."
- `ingest_runs(id, started_at, finished_at, status, stats_json)` — drives the
  dashboard's "last successful ingest" timestamp (shown in UTC, explicitly labeled —
  CR2 later asks for local time; not preempting that here).
- Indexes on `(company_id, store_local_date)` for orders, refunds, and ad_spend.

Ingest is idempotent via `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` (orders, keyed on
`source_order_id`; refunds, keyed on `source_refund_id`) and `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`
keyed on content hash (ad_spend) / `(company, source, record, reason)` (issues) — so
re-running never doubles a count. Every real order and duplicate source row shares the
same natural key, so a second ingest pass is a no-op on row counts and totals.

## Ingest logic (`server/src/ingest/`)

- `orders.js`: for each raw record — if `refund_of` is set, route to refunds
  (amount = `total_refunded`, date = its own `created_at`); else if `created_at` is
  missing, route to `ingest_issues` (`reason: "missing_created_at"`); else upsert into
  `orders` + `line_items`. Byte-duplicate rows (`L-1007`, `H-204`) naturally collapse
  on the `(company_id, source_order_id)` unique key.
- `ads.js`: compute `store_local_date` from `date_start`; if `currency !==
  company.currency`, still insert the row (so it's visible in issues + excludable from
  spend) but flag it and exclude it from the spend aggregate; dedupe by row hash as above.
- `run.js`: CLI entrypoint — ingests both sources for both companies, writes one
  `ingest_runs` row, prints a per-company summary (orders / refunds / ad rows /
  issues counts) to stdout. This stdout is what gets pasted into NOTES.md, run twice
  in a row, to prove idempotency.

## KPI query (`server/src/kpi.js`)

For a company + inclusive date range: generate **every calendar date in the range**
(zero-filled, not a union of days-with-activity — this is the direct fix for bug #7,
stronger than the starter's approach), left-join daily aggregates:
- gross = `SUM(line_items.price_cents * quantity)` for real orders on that date
- refunds = `SUM(refunds.amount_cents)` on that date
- net = gross − refunds
- orders = `COUNT(*)` from `orders` on that date
- spend = `SUM(ad_spend.spend_cents)` where `currency = company.currency`, on that date
- roas = `net / spend` when spend > 0, else `null` → UI renders "—"

## API / server (`server/src/server.js`)

- `GET /d/:token` — looks up the token server-side; unknown/wrong token → real HTTP
  **404** (not the SPA shell), matching the brief's Part A spec (CR2 later asks for
  401 — not implemented now). Valid token → serve the built SPA `index.html`.
- `GET /api/dashboard/:token?start=&end=` — 404 JSON on bad token; otherwise company
  info, KPI totals + daily rows, `last_ingest_at` (UTC), and the `ingest_issues`
  summary for visibility. Defaults to `2026-08-01..2026-08-14`.
- Serves `web/dist` as static assets for everything else.

## Frontend (`web/src/`)

- `App.jsx` — router: `/d/:token` → `DashboardPage`, everything else → simple not-found.
- `DashboardPage.jsx` — fetches `/api/dashboard/:token`, renders company name + range,
  5 KPI cards, a daily table, a small SVG chart (net revenue bars + spend line), the
  "last successful ingest" timestamp, and a compact issues/flags strip (e.g. "$25.00
  USD ad spend excluded — currency mismatch, Aug 12"; "1 order excluded — missing
  date"). 404 response renders a plain "this link is invalid" state.

## Tests (`server/test/`), against the **real fixtures**, asserting the exact numbers
already hand-verified in NOTES.md's corrected KPI table

- `duplicates.test.js` — ingest Lumen/Harbor orders, assert `L-1007`/`H-204` each
  produce exactly 1 order row (Harbor Aug 4 = 1 order, $240.00 gross).
- `refunds.test.js` — Lumen Aug 4 net after `L-1003-R`; Harbor Aug 11 = **$160.00 net**
  (not the starter's $250.00), and the refund lands on Aug 11 (its own date), not Aug 1.
- `timezone.test.js` — `L-1009` buckets to Aug 4 (LA), `H-207` buckets to Aug 8
  (Sydney), not the naive UTC-slice dates.
- `spend_no_orders.test.js` — Lumen/Harbor Aug 9: 0 orders, spend present, day still
  appears in the daily KPI rows with correct spend and ROAS = "—" logic upstream (net=0
  but spend>0 → roas 0, not undefined — distinct from the spend=0 em-dash case).
- `ad_dedup.test.js` — `L-C1` Aug 5 sums both rows to $150.90 (genuine two events), and
  a re-ingest doesn't double it (idempotency on the hash key).
- `currency_mismatch.test.js` — Harbor Aug 12 spend excludes `H-C9`'s $25 USD → $69.00,
  and the row appears in `ingest_issues`.
- `range.test.js` — Aug 14 is included (inclusive range fix for bug #6).
- `idempotency.test.js` — run ingest twice against a temp DB; assert identical row
  counts and identical KPI totals after each run. Output pasted into NOTES.md.
- `totals.test.js` — full-range totals match NOTES.md exactly: Lumen 13 orders /
  $1,750.00 / $192.00 refunds / $1,558.00 net / $771.15 spend / 2.02 ROAS; Harbor 14 /
  $2,580.00 / $80.00 / $2,500.00 / $1,076.10 / 2.32 ROAS.

## One-command run

Root `package.json` (npm workspaces: `server`, `web`):
```
npm install        # once
npm start           # migrate + ingest + build web + serve on http://localhost:4000
```
`npm start` = `npm run ingest --workspace server && npm run build --workspace web && npm run serve --workspace server`. Server prints the two dashboard URLs on boot.

## Docs touched this pass

- `NOTES.md` — append (not replace) "Dedupe rule", "Timezone and refund rules", and
  the pasted idempotency command output. Bottleneck/Failures/CR sections stay out —
  those belong to Part B/C/Day-2.
- `README.md` (new) — one-command run, the two dashboard URLs with their real tokens,
  KPI definitions in my own words.
- `AI-MOMENTS.md` / `CR2-RESPONSE.md` — **not** created this pass (Day-2 deliverables).

## Commits

Baseline commit of the untouched brief/fixtures/starter first, then incremental
commits roughly: repo scaffold + workspaces → schema/migration → orders+refunds
ingest → ad ingest → KPI query layer → API server → React app shell + dashboard UI →
tests → README/NOTES updates. That's naturally 8-10+ commits for Part A alone, each
well under 40% of the final line count since fixtures/schema/frontend/tests are all
separately sized chunks. I'll flag to the user that the "two sessions 8+ hours apart"
timing requirement is on them — I can't fabricate elapsed wall-clock time.

## Verification

- `npm start`, then load both `/d/{lumen-token}` and `/d/{harbor-token}` in the

  ingest timestamp, and issue flags render correctly; hit an invalid token and confirm
  a real 404.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/kurisu/Desktop/2026 Files/Claude Projects/assessment/northstar-takehome-v3/server/src/money.js",
  "content": "// Money is stored as integer minor units (cents) everywhere past ingest, computed\n// by string parsing rather than float arithmetic -- avoids the accumulation drift\n// the starter script was exposed to (native `float` totals, see NOTES.md defect #9).\n\nexport function decimalStringToCents(value) {\n  if (value === null || value === undefined) return 0;\n  const str = String(value).trim();\n  const negative = str.startsWith(\"-\");\n  const unsigned = negative ? str.slice(1) : str;\n  const [whole, frac = \"\"] = unsigned.split(\".\");\n  const fracPadded = (frac + \"00\").slice(0, 2);\n  const cents = Number(whole || \"0\") * 100 + Number(fracPadded || \"0\");\n  return negative ? -cents : cents;\n}\n\nexport function centsToNumber(cents) {\n  return Math.round(cents) / 100;\n}\n\nexport function centsToDisplay(cents) {\n  const sign = cents < 0 ? \"-\" : \"\";\n  const abs = Math.abs(Math.round(cents));\n  const whole = Math.floor(abs / 100);\n  const frac = String(abs % 100).padStart(2, \"0\");\n  return `${sign}${whole}.${frac}`;\n}\n"
}

  "content": "// Store-local calendar date for a company, derived from the record's true instant\n// (Date correctly parses both a `-07:00` offset and a bare `Z`/UTC suffix) rather\n// than string-slicing the first 10 characters of `created_at`, which silently\n// assumes the timestamp is already store-local (see NOTES.md defect #3).\nconst formatterCache = new Map();\n\nfunction formatterFor(timeZone) {\n  let formatter = formatterCache.get(timeZone);\n  if (!formatter) {\n    formatter = new Intl.DateTimeFormat(\"en-US\", {\n      timeZone,\n      year: \"numeric\",\n      month: \"2-digit\",\n      day: \"2-digit\",\n    });\n    formatterCache.set(timeZone, formatter);\n  }\n  return formatter;\n}\n\nexport function toStoreLocalDate(isoTimestamp, timeZone) {\n  if (!isoTimestamp) return null;\n  const date = new Date(isoTimestamp);\n  if (Number.isNaN(date.getTime())) return null;\n  const parts = formatterFor(timeZone).formatToParts(date);\n  const lookup = Object.fromEntries(parts.map((p) => [p.type, p.value]));\n  return `${lookup.year}-${lookup.month}-${lookup.day}`;\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/kurisu/Desktop/2026 Files/Claude Projects/assessment/northstar-takehome-v3/server/src/ingest/orders.js",
  "content": "import { decimalStringToCents } from \"../money.js\";\nimport { toStoreLocalDate } from \"../timezone.js\";\n\n// A record with `refund_of` set is a refund event, not a new order (NOTES.md\n// defect #1) -- it never touches the `orders` table. Two passes: real orders\n// first, then refunds, so a refund can always resolve its parent regardless of\n// file ordering.\nexport function ingestOrders(db, company, records) {\n  const stats = { ordersUpserted: 0, refundsUpserted: 0, issues: 0 };\n  const ingestedAt = new Date().toISOString();\n\n  const upsertOrder = db.prepare(`\n    INSERT INTO orders (company_id, source_order_id, name, created_at_raw, store_local_date, currency, financial_status, total_price_cents, ingested_at)\n    VALUES (@companyId, @sourceOrderId, @name, @createdAtRaw, @storeLocalDate, @currency, @financialStatus, @totalPriceCents, @ingestedAt)\n    ON CONFLICT(company_id, source_order_id) DO UPDATE SET\n      name = excluded.name,\n      created_at_raw = excluded.created_at_raw,\n      store_local_date = excluded.store_local_date,\n      currency = excluded.currency,\n      financial_status = excluded.financial_status,\n      total_price_cents = excluded.total_price_cents,\n      ingested_at = excluded.ingested_at\n    RETURNING id\n  `);\n  const deleteLineItems = db.prepare(`DELETE FROM line_items WHERE order_id = ?`);\n  const insertLineItem = db.prepare(`\n    INSERT INTO line_items (order_id, sku, title, quantity, price_cents) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)\n  `);\n  const upsertRefund = db.prepare(`\n    INSERT INTO refunds (company_id, source_refund_id, order_id, store_local_date, amount_cents, created_at_raw, ingested_at)\n    VALUES (@companyId, @sourceRefundId, @orderId, @storeLocalDate, @amountCents, @createdAtRaw, @ingestedAt)\n    ON CONFLICT(company_id, source_refund_id) DO UPDATE SET\n      order_id = excluded.order_id,\n      store_local_date = excluded.store_local_date,\n      amount_cents = excluded.amount_cents,\n      created_at_raw = excluded.created_at_raw,\n      ingested_at = excluded.ingested_at\n  `);\n  const findOrderId = db.prepare(`SELECT id FROM orders WHERE company_id = ? AND source_order_id = ?`);\n  const recordIssue = db.prepare(`\n    INSERT INTO ingest_issues (company_id, source, source_record_id, reason, detail, detected_at)\n    VALUES (@companyId, 'orders', @sourceRecordId, @reason, @detail, @detectedAt)\n    ON CONFLICT(company_id, source, source_record_id, reason) DO UPDATE SET\n      detail = excluded.detail, detected_at = excluded.detected_at\n  `);\n\n  const run = db.transaction((records) => {\n    const orderRecords = records.filter((r) => !r.refund_of);\n    const refundRecords = records.filter((r) => r.refund_of);\n\n    for (const rec of orderRecords) {\n      if (!rec.created_at) {\n        recordIssue.run({\n          companyId: company.id,\n          sourceRecordId: rec.id ?? null,\n          reason: \"missing_created_at\",\n          detail: \"order missing created_at\",\n          detectedAt: ingestedAt,\n        });\n        stats.issues++;\n        continue;\n      }\n\n      const storeLocalDate = toStoreLocalDate(rec.created_at, company.timezone);\n      const { id: orderId } = upsertOrder.get({\n        companyId: company.id,\n        sourceOrderId: rec.id,\n        name: rec.name ?? null,\n        createdAtRaw: rec.created_at,\n        storeLocalDate,\n        currency: rec.currency ?? company.currency,\n        financialStatus: rec.financial_status ?? null,\n        totalPriceCents: decimalStringToCents(rec.total_price),\n        ingestedAt,\n      });\n\n      deleteLineItems.run(orderId);\n      for (const li of rec.line_items ?? []) {\n        insertLineItem.run(orderId, li.sku ?? null, li.title ?? null, li.quantity ?? 0, decimalStringToCents(li.price));\n      }\n      stats.ordersUpserted++;\n    }\n\n    for (const rec of refundRecords) {\n      if (!rec.created_at) {\n        recordIssue.run({\n          companyId: company.id,\n          sourceRecordId: rec.id ?? null,\n          reason: \"missing_created_at\",\n          detail: \"refund missing created_at\",\n          detectedAt: ingestedAt,\n        });\n        stats.issues++;\n        continue;\n      }\n\n      const storeLocalDate = toStoreLocalDate(rec.created_at, company.timezone);\n      const original = findOrderId.get(company.id, rec.refund_of);\n      if (!original) {\n        recordIssue.run({\n          companyId: company.id,\n          sourceRecordId: rec.id ?? null,\n          reason: \"orphan_refund\",\n          detail: `refund_of \"${rec.refund_of}\" not found among ingested orders`,\n          detectedAt: ingestedAt,\n        });\n      }\n\n      upsertRefund.run({\n        companyId: company.id,\n        sourceRefundId: rec.id,\n        orderId: original ? original.id : null,\n        storeLocalDate,\n        amountCents: decimalStringToCents(rec.total_refunded),\n        createdAtRaw: rec.created_at,\n        ingestedAt,\n      });\n      stats.refundsUpserted++;\n    }\n  });\n\n  run(records);\n  return stats;\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/kurisu/Desktop/2026 Files/Claude Projects/assessment/northstar-takehome-v3/server/src/ingest/ads.js",
  "content": "import crypto from \"node:crypto\";\nimport { decimalStringToCents } from \"../money.js\";\nimport { toStoreLocalDate } from \"../timezone.js\";\n\n// A byte-identical resend must be dropped; two genuinely different spend events for\n// the same campaign/day must both be summed (NOTES.md defect #4 -- the starter's\n// (campaign, date) key conflates the two). We dedupe on a hash of the full row\n// instead, so identity is \"this exact spend event\", not \"this campaign on this day\".\nfunction rowHash(rec) {\n  const canonical = JSON.stringify({\n    campaign_id: rec.campaign_id,\n    date: rec.date,\n    date_start: rec.date_start,\n    spend: rec.spend,\n    currency: rec.currency,\n    impressions: rec.impressions,\n    clicks: rec.clicks,\n  });\n  return crypto.createHash(\"sha256\").update(canonical).digest(\"hex\");\n}\n\nexport function ingestAds(db, company, records) {\n  const stats = { rowsUpserted: 0, rowsSkippedDuplicate: 0, currencyMismatches: 0, issues: 0 };\n  const ingestedAt = new Date().toISOString();\n\n  const insertAd = db.prepare(`\n    INSERT INTO ad_spend (company_id, campaign_id, campaign_name, store_local_date, date_start_utc, spend_cents, currency, impressions, clicks, row_hash, ingested_at)\n    VALUES (@companyId, @campaignId, @campaignName, @storeLocalDate, @dateStartUtc, @spendCents, @currency, @impressions, @clicks, @rowHash, @ingestedAt)\n    ON CONFLICT(company_id, row_hash) DO NOTHING\n  `);\n  const recordIssue = db.prepare(`\n    INSERT INTO ingest_issues (company_id, source, source_record_id, reason, detail, detected_at)\n    VALUES (@companyId, 'ads', @sourceRecordId, @reason, @detail, @detectedAt)\n    ON CONFLICT(company_id, source, source_record_id, reason) DO UPDATE SET\n      detail = excluded.detail, detected_at = excluded.detected_at\n  `);\n\n  const run = db.transaction((records) => {\n    for (const rec of records) {\n      if (!rec.date_start) {\n        recordIssue.run({\n          companyId: company.id,\n          sourceRecordId: rec.campaign_id ?? null,\n          reason: \"missing_date_start\",\n          detail: \"ad row missing date_start\",\n          detectedAt: ingestedAt,\n        });\n        stats.issues++;\n        continue;\n      }\n\n      const storeLocalDate = toStoreLocalDate(rec.date_start, company.timezone);\n      const hash = rowHash(rec);\n      const result = insertAd.run({\n        companyId: company.id,\n        campaignId: rec.campaign_id,\n        campaignName: rec.campaign_name ?? null,\n        storeLocalDate,\n        dateStartUtc: rec.date_start,\n        spendCents: decimalStringToCents(rec.spend),\n        currency: rec.currency ?? company.currency,\n        impressions: rec.impressions ?? null,\n        clicks: rec.clicks ?? null,\n        rowHash: hash,\n        ingestedAt,\n      });\n\n      if (result.changes === 0) {\n        stats.rowsSkippedDuplicate++;\n        continue;\n      }\n      stats.rowsUpserted++;\n\n      if (rec.currency && rec.currency !== company.currency) {\n        stats.currencyMismatches++;\n        recordIssue.run({\n          companyId: company.id,\n          sourceRecordId: `${rec.campaign_id}:${rec.date}`,\n          reason: \"currency_mismatch\",\n          detail: `ad row currency ${rec.currency} does not match company currency ${company.currency}; excluded from spend total`,\n          detectedAt: ingestedAt,\n        });\n      }\n    }\n  });\n\n  run(records);\n  return stats;\n}\n"
}

  "content": "import { openDb } from \"../db.js\";\nimport { migrate } from \"../migrate.js\";\nimport { COMPANIES } from \"../companies.js\";\nimport { ingestOrders } from \"./orders.js\";\nimport { ingestAds } from \"./ads.js\";\nimport { loadOrders, loadAds } from \"./loadFixtures.js\";\n\n// Re-running this end to end is idempotent: every table's natural key (source\n// order/refund id, or ad-row content hash) means a second pass upserts the same\n// rows to the same values rather than creating new ones. See NOTES.md for the\n// paired \"run twice\" output that proves it.\nexport function ingestAll(db) {\n  migrate(db);\n\n  const startedAt = new Date().toISOString();\n  const insertRun = db.prepare(\n    `INSERT INTO ingest_runs (started_at, finished_at, status, stats_json) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)`\n  );\n\n  const getCompany = db.prepare(`SELECT * FROM companies WHERE slug = ?`);\n  const perCompany = {};\n\n  try {\n    for (const cfg of Object.values(COMPANIES)) {\n      const company = getCompany.get(cfg.slug);\n      const orderRecords = loadOrders(cfg.slug);\n      const adRecords = loadAds(cfg.slug);\n\n      const orderStats = ingestOrders(db, company, orderRecords);\n      const adStats = ingestAds(db, company, adRecords);\n\n      perCompany[cfg.slug] = {\n        company: company.name,\n        sourceOrderRecords: orderRecords.length,\n        sourceAdRecords: adRecords.length,\n        ...orderStats,\n        ...adStats,\n      };\n    }\n\n    const finishedAt = new Date().toISOString();\n    insertRun.run(startedAt, finishedAt, \"success\", JSON.stringify(perCompany));\n    return { status: \"success\", startedAt, finishedAt, perCompany };\n  } catch (err) {\n    const finishedAt = new Date().toISOString();\n    insertRun.run(startedAt, finishedAt, \"failed\", JSON.stringify({ error: String(err) }));\n    throw err;\n  }\n}\n\nif (import.meta.url === `file://${process.argv[1]}`) {\n  const db = openDb();\n  const result = ingestAll(db);\n  console.log(`Ingest ${result.status} — started ${result.startedAt}, finished ${result.finishedAt}`);\n  for (const [slug, stats] of Object.entries(result.perCompany)) {\n    console.log(`\\n${stats.company} (${slug})`);\n    console.log(`  source: ${stats.sourceOrderRecords} order records, ${stats.sourceAdRecords} ad records`);\n    console.log(`  orders upserted:   ${stats.ordersUpserted}`);\n    console.log(`  refunds upserted:  ${stats.refundsUpserted}`);\n    console.log(`  ad rows upserted:  ${stats.rowsUpserted}`);\n    console.log(`  ad rows skipped (exact duplicate): ${stats.rowsSkippedDuplicate}`);\n    console.log(`  ad rows currency mismatch: ${stats.currencyMismatches}`);\n    console.log(`  issues logged: ${stats.issues + (stats.currencyMismatches ?? 0)}`);\n  }\n  console.log(\"\\nDashboard URLs:\");\n  for (const cfg of Object.values(COMPANIES)) {\n    console.log(`  ${cfg.name}: http://localhost:${process.env.PORT || 4000}/d/${cfg.dashboardToken}`);\n  }\n  db.close();\n}\n"

{
  "file_path": "/Users/kurisu/Desktop/2026 Files/Claude Projects/assessment/northstar-takehome-v3/server/src/kpi.js",
  "content": "import { centsToNumber } from \"./money.js\";\n\nfunction enumerateDates(start, end) {\n  const dates = [];\n  let cursor = new Date(`${start}T00:00:00Z`);\n  const last = new Date(`${end}T00:00:00Z`);\n  while (cursor <= last) {\n    dates.push(cursor.toISOString().slice(0, 10));\n    cursor = new Date(cursor.getTime() + 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000);\n  }\n  return dates;\n}\n\nfunction toMap(rows, key = \"date\", value = \"cents\") {\n  const map = new Map();\n  for (const row of rows) map.set(row[key], row[value] ?? 0);\n  return map;\n}\n\nexport function getCompanyByToken(db, token) {\n  return db.prepare(`SELECT * FROM companies WHERE dashboard_token = ?`).get(token);\n}\n\n// Every calendar day in [start, end] gets a row, zero-filled -- not just the days\n// that happen to have an order or a spend record. This is what makes a day with ad\n// spend and zero orders show up correctly (NOTES.md defect #7), rather than only\n// appearing when it happens to intersect the set of days with orders.\nexport function getDashboardData(db, company, { start, end }) {\n  const dates = enumerateDates(start, end);\n\n  const grossRows = db\n    .prepare(\n      `SELECT o.store_local_date AS date, SUM(li.price_cents * li.quantity) AS cents\n       FROM line_items li JOIN orders o ON o.id = li.order_id\n       WHERE o.company_id = ? AND o.store_local_date BETWEEN ? AND ?\n       GROUP BY o.store_local_date`\n    )\n    .all(company.id, start, end);\n\n  const orderCountRows = db\n    .prepare(\n      `SELECT store_local_date AS date, COUNT(*) AS cnt\n       FROM orders\n       WHERE company_id = ? AND store_local_date BETWEEN ? AND ?\n       GROUP BY store_local_date`\n    )\n    .all(company.id, start, end);\n\n  const refundRows = db\n    .prepare(\n      `SELECT store_local_date AS date, SUM(amount_cents) AS cents\n       FROM refunds\n       WHERE company_id = ? AND store_local_date BETWEEN ? AND ?\n       GROUP BY store_local_date`\n    )\n    .all(company.id, start, end);\n\n  const spendRows = db\n    .prepare(\n      `SELECT store_local_date AS date, SUM(spend_cents) AS cents\n       FROM ad_spend\n       WHERE company_id = ? AND currency = ? AND store_local_date BETWEEN ? AND ?\n       GROUP BY store_local_date`\n    )\n    .all(company.id, company.currency, start, end);\n\n  const excludedSpendRows = db\n    .prepare(\n      `SELECT store_local_date AS date, currency, SUM(spend_cents) AS cents\n       FROM ad_spend\n       WHERE company_id = ? AND currency != ? AND store_local_date BETWEEN ? AND ?\n       GROUP BY store_local_date, currency`\n    )\n    .all(company.id, company.currency, start, end);\n\n  const grossMap = toMap(grossRows);\n  const orderCountMap = toMap(orderCountRows, \"date\", \"cnt\");\n  const refundMap = toMap(refundRows);\n  const spendMap = toMap(spendRows);\n\n  const daily = dates.map((date) => {\n    const grossCents = grossMap.get(date) ?? 0;\n    const refundCents = refundMap.get(date) ?? 0;\n    const spendCents = spendMap.get(date) ?? 0;\n    const netCents = grossCents - refundCents;\n    return {\n      date,\n      orders: orderCountMap.get(date) ?? 0,\n      grossSales: centsToNumber(grossCents),\n      netRevenue: centsToNumber(netCents),\n      refunds: centsToNumber(refundCents),\n      adSpend: centsToNumber(spendCents),\n      roas: spendCents > 0 ? netCents / spendCents : null,\n    };\n  });\n\n  const totals = daily.reduce(\n    (acc, row) => {\n      acc.orders += row.orders;\n      acc.grossSales += row.grossSales;\n      acc.netRevenue += row.netRevenue;\n      acc.refunds += row.refunds;\n      acc.adSpend += row.adSpend;\n      return acc;\n    },\n    { orders: 0, grossSales: 0, netRevenue: 0, refunds: 0, adSpend: 0 }\n  );\n  totals.roas = totals.adSpend > 0 ? totals.netRevenue / totals.adSpend : null;\n\n  const lastRun = db\n    .prepare(`SELECT finished_at FROM ingest_runs WHERE status = 'success' ORDER BY finished_at DESC LIMIT 1`)\n    .get();\n\n  const issues = db\n    .prepare(`SELECT source, source_record_id, reason, detail, detected_at FROM ingest_issues WHERE company_id = ? ORDER BY detected_at DESC`)\n    .all(company.id);\n\n  return {\n    company: {\n      name: company.name,\n      currency: company.currency,\n      timezone: company.timezone,\n    },\n    range: { start, end },\n    daily,\n    totals,\n    lastIngestAt: lastRun ? lastRun.finished_at : null,\n    issues,\n    excludedForeignSpend: excludedSpendRows.map((r) => ({\n      date: r.date,\n      currency: r.currency,\n      amount: centsToNumber(r.cents),\n    })),\n  };\n}\n"
}

  "content": "import test from \"node:test\";\nimport assert from \"node:assert/strict\";\nimport { freshIngestedDb, reingest } from \"./helpers.js\";\nimport { getDashboardData } from \"../src/kpi.js\";\n\nconst range = { start: \"2026-08-01\", end: \"2026-08-14\" };\n\nfunction rowCounts(db) {\n  return {\n    orders: db.prepare(`SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM orders`).get().n,\n    lineItems: db.prepare(`SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM line_items`).get().n,\n    refunds: db.prepare(`SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM refunds`).get().n,\n    adSpend: db.prepare(`SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM ad_spend`).get().n,\n    issues: db.prepare(`SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM ingest_issues`).get().n,\n  };\n}\n\n// Re-running ingest against the same source files must be a no-op on both row\n// counts and KPI totals -- every table's natural key (source order/refund id, or\n// ad-row content hash) makes a second pass an upsert onto the same rows, not new\n// inserts. }

{
  "file_path": "/Users/kurisu/Desktop/2026 Files/Claude Projects/assessment/northstar-takehome-v3/web/src/components/DailyChart.jsx",
  "content": "// Hand-rolled SVG chart -- no charting dependency. Net revenue as bars, ad spend\n// as an overlaid line, both scaled to the same axis so the relationship between\n// them (including a spend-only day: a line point with no bar under it) is visible.\nexport default function DailyChart({ daily }) {\n  const width = 760;\n  const height = 220;\n  const padding = 36;\n  const innerHeight = height - padding * 2;\n  const max = Math.max(1, ...daily.map((d) => Math.max(d.netRevenue, d.adSpend)));\n  const barWidth = (width - padding * 2) / daily.length;\n  const scaleY = (v) => height - padding - (Math.max(v, 0) / max) * innerHeight;\n\n  const linePoints = daily\n    .map((d, i) => `${padding + i * barWidth + barWidth / 2},${scaleY(d.adSpend)}`)\n    .join(\" \");\n\n  return (\n    <svg viewBox={`0 0 ${width} ${height}`} className=\"daily-chart\" role=\"img\" aria-label=\"Daily net revenue and ad spend\">\n      <line x1={padding} y1={height - padding} x2={width - padding} y2={height - padding} className=\"chart-axis\" />\n      {daily.map((d, i) => {\n        const barHeight = (Math.max(d.netRevenue, 0) / max) * innerHeight;\n        return (\n          <rect\n            key={d.date}\n            x={padding + i * barWidth + barWidth * 0.15}\n            y={height - padding - barHeight}\n            width={barWidth * 0.7}\n            height={barHeight}\n            className=\"chart-bar\"\n          >\n            <title>{`${d.date}: net ${d.netRevenue.toFixed(2)}, spend ${d.adSpend.toFixed(2)}`}</title>\n          </rect>\n        );\n      })}\n      <polyline points={linePoints} className=\"chart-line\" />\n      {daily.map((d, i) => (\n        <circle\n          key={`${d.date}-dot`}\n          cx={padding + i * barWidth + barWidth / 2}\n          cy={scaleY(d.adSpend)}\n          r={2.5}\n          className=\"chart-dot\"\n        />\n      ))}\n      <text x={padding} y={16} className=\"chart-legend\">\n        ▮ net revenue &nbsp;&nbsp; ─ ad spend\n      </text>\n    </svg>\n  );\n}\n"
}
